How answers are graded

- Each attempt is compared with your answer and marked right or wrong. The grade is on the whole attempt; the final answer shown is picked out automatically as a guide.
- Numbers match when they are the same value written differently: 0.675, 67.5% and 67.5 percentage points all match, and so do different scales (thousand, million, billion), thousands separators, currency symbols and numbers written as words.

- For an ordered list, a title, honorific or article added to an entry is accepted if the order is right either with it or without it.
- Only the value the attempt finally commits to counts. Values reached while working, or considered and set aside, do not. An attempt that offers several values without choosing one is wrong, even if one of them is right.

Full answer
-----------

The 2019 hospice counts appear in Document 5, Table 1:

- Arizona: 152
- California: 1,130
- Nevada: 55
- Texas: 718

Leaving California out, the total is: 152 + 55 + 718 = 925

In Document 6, CMS names states where home health agencies share an address: Ohio, Texas, Michigan, North Carolina, and Nevada.

Of the 2019 hospice-total states (excluding California), the ones also named are Texas (718) and Nevada (55).

Combined: 718 + 55 = 773

Percentage: 773 ÷ 925 = 0.835675… → 83.57%

- Arizona: 152

- Nevada: 55
- Texas: 718
- Georgia: 221 (in text)

The states named for home-health agencies sharing an address are Ohio, Texas, Michigan, North Carolina, and Nevada. Among the states with 2019 hospice totals (excluding California), those in that list are Texas (718) and Nevada (55).

Combined: 718 + 55 = 773.

Percentage: 773 / 1,146 = 0.67452... = 67.45%.

55 + 718 = 773

773 ÷ 1,146 = 0.674520… = 67.45%

Finding the relevant data

- Michigan – 485
- Texas – 2,269

2. Why "leave California out." The notice also discusses Los Angeles County, stating the county's HHA count "rose more than 40 percent" between 2019 and 2023, with "well over 1,000 new HHAs" enrolled since 2019 (i.e., an implied California‑area 2019 base). California is not among the states identified for co‑located HHAs, so the question directs us to set it aside. Excluding it leaves only Michigan and Texas.

3. States CMS names for HHAs sharing an address. Document 5, § II.B:

> "CMS has recently detected several situations in Ohio where numerous HHAs are operating out of a single, common practice location... Similar situations have been seen in Texas, Michigan, North Carolina, and Nevada."

Named states = Ohio, Texas, Michigan, North Carolina, Nevada.

Calculation

- Total 2019 (California excluded): 485 (MI) + 2,269 (TX) = 2,754
- Of these, in the co‑location states: Michigan (485) + Texas (2,269) = 2,754, since both Michigan and Texas are on the co‑location list

$$\frac{2{,}754}{2{,}754}\times 100 = 100.00\%$$

Final answer

100.00 %

-----------

Step 1 – Identify the 2019 hospice totals CMS provides

| State | CY 2019 | |---|---| | Arizona | 152 | | California | 1,130 | | Nevada | 55 | | Texas | 718 |

Step 2 – Build the denominator ("leaving California out")

152 (AZ) + 55 (NV) + 718 (TX) + 221 (GA) = 1,146

Step 3 – Identify the "address" states

CMS's statements about entities sharing an address appear in Doc. 5:

- California: "numerous hospices were simultaneously operating out of a single building" in Los Angeles County (this is also the state whose new enrollees footnote 25 mistakenly labels "new HHAs in California").
- Ohio: "instances of numerous hospices operating out of a single location."

These are the only two States CMS names for address-sharing. (Georgia is named only for rapid growth; Arizona, Nevada and Texas are named for non‑operational addresses, not shared ones; and no State is named for home health agencies sharing an address apart from the California passage.)

Step 4 – Count the qualifying hospices in the remaining States

Of Arizona (152), Nevada (55), Texas (718) and Georgia (221), none is a State CMS names for address-sharing. Ohio – the only other such State – has no 2019 total given.

Numerator = 0 Denominator = 1,146

0 ÷ 1,146 = 0

0.00 %

Their combined 2019 hospice total: 718 + 55 = 773

Percentage: 773 / 925 × 100 = 83.57%

The home health agencies lost exceeded the hospices gained by 367.

Full answer

The documents provide state-level enrollment counts for 2019 and 2025 for both provider types.

Home Health Agencies (Document 6, footnote 23)

- Michigan: 485 → 361 = 124 lost
- Texas: 2,269 → 1,893 = 376 lost

Total HHA lost = 124 + 376 = 500

Hospices (Document 5, Table 1 and text)

- Nevada: 55 (2019) → 188 (2025) = 133 gained
- Georgia: 221 (2019) → 273 (2025) = 52 gained

Total hospice gained = 133 + 52 = 185

Difference 500 − 185 = 315

So the home health agencies lost exceeded the hospices gained by 315.

Home health agencies (2019 → 2025) – states with reported totals for both years:

- Michigan: 485 → 361 = 124 lost
- Texas: 2,269 → 1,893 = 376 lost
- Total HHA losses: 500

Hospices (2019 → 2025) – states with reported totals for both years:

So, the home health agencies lost exceeded the hospices gained by 367.

Full answer

Identifying the Relevant Data

HHAs with 2019 and 2025 enrolled totals (Document 6, footnote 23):

- Michigan: 485 (2019) → 361 (2025) = 124 lost
- Texas: 2,269 (2019) → 1,893 (2025) = 376 lost

Total HHAs lost: 124 + 376 = 500

Hospices with 2019 and 2025 enrolled totals (Document 5):

- Nevada: 55 (2019, Table 1) → 188 (2025, §II.C text) = 133 gained
- Georgia: 221 (2019) → 273 (2025, §II.C text) = 52 gained

Total hospices gained: 133 + 52 = 185

Calculation

500 (HHAs lost) − 185 (hospices gained) = 315

The home health agencies lost exceeded the hospices gained by 315.
